# Known issues

Every row here is work this project WILL do. There is no not-going-to-fix status
and no deferral: a divergence from Linux is either fixed or it is an open row
waiting for its lane. A row that records a shape we chose on purpose still names
the work that makes it right, because "we meant to" is not a reason to leave
Linux behaviour unimplemented.

**This file is the only store of live work.** Any lane that finds an issue adds
its row HERE, to the section it belongs to, in the same PR that finds it. Any
lane that fixes one flips it to `FIXED <sha>` and MOVES it to
`scratch/fixed-issues.md` in the same PR. Rows are never deleted, only
relocated.

Per-lane drop files under `scratch/issues.d/` are abolished and the directory is
gone (C300 folded the last 77 rows back in). A row parked in a side file is a row
nobody reads: the fold step was skipped often enough that a third of the ledger
lived outside it, and six lanes each filed the same aarch64 stack-ceiling defect
without seeing the other five. A concurrent-lane conflict on this file is a
rebase round-trip and is worth it — resolve it by taking BOTH sides' rows, never
by dropping one.

Before filing, read the section your row belongs to. If a row already states the
fact, add your evidence to it rather than filing a second row: one fact, one row.

`Status`: `OPEN` | `IN-PROGRESS <branch>` | `FIXED <sha>`.

`Owner`: `NEXT` is the queue head. Those rows are claimed before any unowned
work and become `IN-PROGRESS <branch>` when the current integration finishes.

`Class` says what KIND of work the row needs, never whether it gets done:

| Class | Means |
|---|---|

`Sev`: `blocker` (merge gate) | `high` (wrong answer reaching userspace) |
`med` (missing surface) | `low` (hygiene, tooling, cosmetics).

## Open-work summary

This is a derived view of the live `OPEN` and `IN-PROGRESS` rows below, not a
second ledger. Regenerate it — never hand-adjust it — in the same change
whenever a row is added, moved, or reclassified:

```
awk -F'|' '/^\| *(OPEN|IN-PROGRESS)/{c=$3;s=toupper($4);gsub(/ /,"",c);gsub(/ /,"",s);print c" "s}' \
  scratch/known_issues.md | sort | uniq -c
```

Hand-adjustment is what made the previous table wrong by 70 rows: it read 339
against an actual 409, and understated `MISSING high` and `INFRA high` by
exactly the rows a lane would have picked up first. D551 regenerated it.

| Class \ Sev | blocker | high | med | low | Total |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| `DEFECT` | 6 | 12 | 38 | 53 | 109 |
| `MISSING` | 1 | 24 | 82 | 69 | 176 |
| `COVERAGE` | 0 | 6 | 52 | 50 | 108 |
| `INFRA` | 0 | 1 | 24 | 32 | 57 |
| **Total** | **7** | **43** | **196** | **204** | **450** |

Never delete a row to make the list look shorter. A row with no owner is still a
row. Retired rows and folded duplicates live in `scratch/fixed-issues.md`.

| OPEN | COVERAGE | low | An out-of-window segment for a half-open request is answered with an acknowledgement, and a SYN repeat with a SYN-ACK, on EVERY arrival: the reference rate-limits both answers against a per-request stamp and counts the skipped ones, so a peer replaying one segment cannot make a listener emit one answer per replay. | OPEN | MISSING | low | **No device in this tree can place received payload in a caller's registered area, so a zero-copy receive registration naming one answers `EOPNOTSUPP`.** F855 re-verified F852's virtio-net analysis against the reference and it is CORRECT, with one addition that changes what the row means: the reference itself refuses this device. Upstream virtio-net declares NO queue-management operations at all, so the reference's own binding ladder stops at its first rung — `EOPNOTSUPP` — for exactly this device. Only a small set of devices carry the capability upstream (a handful of high-end NIC families plus one software test device); none of them is modelled here. This row is therefore NOT a divergence to repair but an absent device class to add: what closes it is a receive engine that can place payload in its own buffer, i.e. a NIC model that carries the capability or a software device built for it. Everything on this side of the contract is already implemented and hosted-tested against a device that DOES declare the capability. | F844, F852, F855. `netdev/rx_queue.rs admit_mp_open`; `netdev::rx_queue::tests::a_capable_device_really_binds_and_the_queue_draws_from_the_provider` and the six binding tests beside it. | unowned |
| OPEN | MISSING | low | **`IORING_OP_RECV_ZC` delivers every byte through the copy path, never by reference — and that is what the reference does in every configuration reachable here.** F855 re-verified and CORRECTED the cause a SECOND time; both earlier statements of it were wrong. It is not `Pkt` linearity (F844's claim) and it is not `RecvBuf::push_payload` copying at input time (F852's claim). The deciding fact is in the ABI: a by-reference completion reports a byte OFFSET INTO THE CALLER'S REGISTERED AREA, so a payload can only be delivered by reference if it physically sits in that area. Nothing but a device writing into the bound queue's buffers puts it there. The reference agrees exactly — it delivers by reference only when the fragment is a pool buffer belonging to THIS instance's own provider, copies unconditionally otherwise, and a registration made without a device has no pool at all, so every one of its deliveries is a copy. Rewriting the stream receive queue to hold buffer references would therefore not produce a single by-reference delivery; it would be machinery with no caller. This row is blocked on, and only on, the device row above. What IS reachable was fixed in F855: the copy path's accounting. | F844, F852, F855. `io_uring/zcrx/recv.rs copy_chunk` over `io_uring_abi::zcrx::copy_run`; `abi/zcrx/tests_copy.rs`. | unowned |
| FIXED F855 (errno) / OPEN (framework) | MISSING | low | **`IORING_ZCRX_AREA_DMABUF` reported `EOPNOTSUPP`; the reference answers `EINVAL`, and F855 corrects it.** Every rung a shared-buffer area meets answers `EINVAL` in the reference: such an area described with no device bound, such an area carrying an offset, and such an area on a kernel built without a buffer-sharing framework. This kernel is the last of those, so `EINVAL` is not an approximation — it is the answer the reference gives for this build. The flag stays RECOGNISED so a caller can tell "this kernel does not know that bit" from "this kernel cannot import an area described that way". The framework itself stays unbuilt, and F855 adds two reference facts to F852's costing that make the ordering firmer: a shared-buffer area REQUIRES a bound device (a device-less registration refuses it outright), and it is not kernel-readable, so the copy fallback is disabled for it — meaning even a completed framework would have no reachable consumer until the device row above closes. Ordering remains: a producing driver, then a capable device, then the framework, then this flag. | F844, F852, F855. `io_uring_abi::zcrx::admit_area_reg`; `a_buffer_sharing_area_is_refused_ahead_of_the_plain_area_rungs`, `a_shared_buffer_area_is_recognised_and_reported_as_an_invalid_description`. | unowned |
| FIXED F852 | MISSING | low | **`ZCRX_REG_IMPORT` and `ZCRX_CTRL_EXPORT` reported `ENXIO`/`EOPNOTSUPP`.** Closed by F852 and left OPEN by oversight; F855 flips it after checking the code rather than the row. An export hands out a descriptor naming one instance and an import adopts it into a second ring under that ring's own id, on a two-count lifetime — the owning handle keeps the instance allocated while a separate user count decides when the device queue closes and the caller's buffers are reclaimed. | F852, F855. `io_uring/register/zcrx.rs` `import`/`export`; `io_uring/zcrx/box_fd.rs`; `abi/zcrx/hold.rs`. | io_uring lane |
| FIXED F855 | DEFECT | low | **The zero-copy receive copy path counted BUFFERS as copy operations and posted a shortage notification the allocation path already posts.** The statistics record a caller maps counts fallbacks; counting the buffers a fallback happened to need made the number a function of the buffer size the caller registered rather than of how often the stack copied. Separately, a run that exhausted the freelist posted `ZCRX_NOTIF_NO_BUFFERS` from the receive path as well as from the provider's allocation path, telling a caller the same shortage twice per receive, and reported zero bytes where the reference reports `ENOMEM` — indistinguishable, to the caller, from a socket with nothing on it. The decision moved into the ungated `io_uring_abi::zcrx::copy_run`; the gated slot is now buffer handling only. | F855. `abi/zcrx/copy.rs`; eight tests in `abi/zcrx/tests_copy.rs`, each with a positive control (per-buffer counting, shortage notification, and zero-instead-of-ENOMEM each reinstated RED and restored GREEN). | io_uring lane |
| OPEN | DEFECT | low | **A zero-copy receive charges the caller's buffer reference BEFORE posting the completion, where the reference charges it after.** Deliberate and forced by a different publication model: this ring publishes a completion's tail inside `post_cqe`, so a caller can observe the completion and hand the buffer back through the refill queue before the kernel has recorded that it owns it, and the return would be dropped as a buffer it never held. The reference batches its tail publication past the point it takes the reference, so the two orders are equivalent there and not here. Recorded so it is visible rather than living only in a code comment; it closes for real if completion publication ever becomes batched. | F855. `io_uring/zcrx/recv.rs queue_cqe`; `io_uring/cqe.rs post_cqe` stores the tail before returning. | io_uring lane |
| FIXED F848 | MISSING | low | `IORING_SETUP_CQE_MIXED` is implemented: the array stays 16 bytes and a 32-byte completion takes TWO adjacent slots carrying `IORING_CQE_F_32`, with an `IORING_CQE_F_SKIP` filler in the last slot when one would otherwise straddle the wrap. `IORING_OP_NOP`'s 32-byte-completion request is the producer a caller can drive it with. | F848. `io_uring_abi::cqe_slot` (12 hosted tests) + `io_uring_abi::nop`; `every_setup_flag_is_either_implemented_or_refused` records it implemented. Positive control: dropping the wrap filler turned 2 tests RED; restored GREEN. | F848 |

| OPEN | DEFECT | high | **`mremap` relocates a mapping by COPYING ITS BYTES; the reference relocates its PAGE TABLE ENTRIES.** `move_vma`/`move_page_tables`/`move_ptes` unlink the source's PTEs and re-link them under the destination's tables, so a move is O(page tables) and transfers no data at all. `mremap_full` instead reads the source and writes the destination through user addresses, which (a) costs O(bytes), (b) MATERIALISES every page of the source range — a sparse mapping arrives at the destination fully populated, where the reference moves only the entries that exist — and (c) is the reason the operation has any user access to lose in the first place. | OPEN | DEFECT | high | **No lock is held across `mremap`, where the reference holds the mm write lock for the whole operation.** `mmap_write_lock_killable(mm)` is taken by the reference's syscall entry and released only at the end, with `vma_start_write` on the source on top; here `find_vma`, `mmap_preserving_prot` and `munmap` each take and RELEASE `AddressSpace::vmas` independently, so the VMA snapshot the checks approved is already stale when the transfer runs. | FIXED B2030 | DEFECT | med | **`modify_ldt` does not IPI the other CPUs running the address space.** The reference's `install_ldt` calls `flush_ldt` on every CPU in `mm_cpumask`, so a sibling thread sees a new descriptor before the installing thread's syscall returns. This port had no general cross-CPU call, so a sibling converged only at its NEXT return to user mode; inside that window a selector above the sibling's loaded limit faults. **B2030 built the missing primitive** — a real per-(sender,target) call-descriptor queue on the machine's single cross-CPU vector (`cpu::call_fn`, `arch_irq::call_fn`, `hal::smp_call`) — and `sched::ldt::install_entry` now converges every CPU in the mm's cpumask, waiting, before the syscall returns. The TLB shootdown rides the same queue rather than keeping its own single-slot protocol, so there is one cross-CPU mechanism, as upstream has. | B2030. Ordering pinned by `sched::ldt::converge::tests` (5); queue protocol by `cpu::call_fn::tests` (22); positive controls: freeing before the converge, dropping the drain reversal, releasing a slot before its handler ran, and converging a fixed mask each turned the matching test RED. | Chris Watkins |
| FIXED B2030 | DEFECT | low | **An LDT is allocated at full size (64 KiB) and never reallocated**, where the reference sizes the allocation to the highest entry in use and swaps the table on each grow. | FIXED F851 | DEFECT | med | virtio-blk's completion poll drained the SAME virtqueue the completion interrupt drives, so a polled ring saved the wait but not the interrupt. The whole ring shadow is per-queue (`BlkQueue` owns the resource, the `RingShadow` and the lock), and the two drain contexts now PARTITION the queues: the completion softirq drains only queues whose completions the device signals, a poll drains only the interrupt-free ones. A transfer submitted through the direct path is stamped `polled` and issued on the poll queue; `can_poll` is true only when such a queue exists, so a single-queue device reports no poll capability rather than promising a saving it cannot make. The QEMU launchers pass `num-queues=2` on every virtio-blk device, since one queue is the default and the negotiation would otherwise find nothing to negotiate. | F851. 15 new hosted tests across `drv-virtio-blk` (33→49) and `virtio` (58→61) plus 2 in `block` (85→87): queue-count arithmetic, the tail placement, the never-take-the-last-queue rule, the `avail.flags` suppression store, the softirq/poll partition, the capability gate and the direct path's `polled` stamp. Positive controls, each RED then restored GREEN: cursor not advancing (2 RED), poll queue allowed to take the last default queue (3 RED), suppression store removed (1 RED), `can_poll` hardcoded true (1 RED), softirq draining every queue (1 RED), direct path not stamping `polled` (1 RED), optional queue withheld from the child (1 RED), poll queue given a completion handler (1 RED). ## Kernel core

| Status | Class | Sev | Issue | Evidence | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| OPEN | MISSING | MED | The aarch64 crash image's device tree is derived from the RUNNING kernel's tree unchanged, so its `/memory` node describes the whole machine and carries no `linux,usable-memory-range`. A crash kernel booted from it would believe it owns all of RAM, including the memory holding the very state a dump exists to capture. Placement is already confined to the reservation, so only what the new kernel is TOLD is wrong — the same defect the x86 side had, in the other arch's handover format. `LoadCtx::system` now carries exactly the map such a fix consumes. Not fixed here: aarch64 `kexec -l` is blocked earlier by the boot device tree, so no arm crash boot can be driven to check it. | B2048. `kexec::file_load::arm_image::assemble::load` passes `ctx.fdt` to `handover::setup_fdt` with no memory rewrite; `ctx.system` is unread on that arch. | unowned |
| OPEN | MISSING | MED | The first 640 KiB of the running kernel's memory is not copied aside before a crash kernel is given that window to use. The crash kernel now receives the low window in its memory map (it cannot boot without one) and will overwrite it, so those bytes are lost to any dump taken afterwards. The reference backs the range up into the reserved region and points the dump at the copy. Nothing consumes it today — there is no `/proc/vmcore` — so this is invisible until the dump surface exists, which is exactly when it would be discovered the hard way. | B2048. `kexec::file_load::kbuf::system_ranges` hands the low window over with no backup step anywhere. | unowned |
| OPEN | MISSING | MED | `kexec_file_load` does not build the ELF core headers that describe the OLD kernel's memory, and passes no `elfcorehdr=` to the crash kernel. The reference constructs them in the kernel for a file-loaded crash image. Without them a crash kernel that boots has no way to find what it is supposed to dump. Not needed for the crash kernel to COME UP, which is why it is separate from the boot itself. | B2048. `kexec::file_load::kexec_file_load` appends nothing to the command line; no `/proc/vmcore` exists to read the headers either. | unowned |
| FIXED B2048 | DEFECT | HIGH | **A crash kernel was entered and then said nothing.** Cause: where a buffer may be PLACED and what the new kernel is TOLD it has were the same map. Confining placement to the reservation is right, but the same map then became the new kernel's memory map, so the crash kernel was handed a machine whose lowest byte of RAM is a reserved window two gigabytes up — and the x86 boot path needs memory below the first megabyte. It was entered and died before it could reach a console, which is why the log stopped at the jump. The two contracts are now two maps: placement is the reservation; the map handed over is that window plus the low-memory one, clipped to memory that exists. **A crash kernel now boots to userspace on x86_64.** | B2048. `scratch/evidence/kexec-x86-crash-boot.log`, whole: panic, machine stop, `kexec: starting new kernel`, the relocated kernel's own banner, `Run /bin/sh as init process`, and a shell prompt. `kexec::file_load::kbuf::{placement_ranges,system_ranges}` (8 tests); positive control — one map for both contracts again — turns 2 RED, restored GREEN. | OPEN | DEFECT | high | **A uniprocessor x86_64 boot wedges in early userspace: a task spins forever in a kernel spinlock whose owner is not running.** Measured with the boot gate's own liveness probe on one prebuilt image: `-smp 1` answers 2/5 boots, `-smp 2` answers 3/3. A wedged guest stops at the same place every time — right after journald's runtime-journal flush, while udev workers and systemd are opening devices — and never switches task again (`[WATCHDOG] soft lockup: no reschedule for 10s`, then `no-progress: 0 context switches for 40s`, with `switches=` identical in both dumps). Two independent captures name the stall site exactly: a `sys_openat` of a console device parked in `Spinlock::lock_irqsave::<PortInner<VtConsoleDriver>, Tty>` from `TtyStruct::open_inner`, and a `block::registry::open_by_dev` parked in `Spinlock::lock::<Vec<Arc<Disk>>, Devices>`. The owner is off-CPU in both: with one CPU nothing can run it, and the spinner holds interrupts masked so no tick, no wakeup and no reschedule can reach it. A second CPU picks the owner up, which is why every gate in the tree — all of which run `-smp 2` — passes. Not yet root-caused: the specific point at which an owner gives up the CPU while holding the lock is unidentified. Ruled out with evidence, so the next lane need not retry them: cross-CPU call/TLB-shootdown wait (`call_function_many` returns immediately when `online_count() <= 1`, so no shootdown is ever issued); a reschedule taken from `preempt_enable`/`preempt_check_resched` with interrupts masked (fixed on this branch and measured — post-fix `-smp 1` is 1/5, unchanged within the sample). | B2047. Captures under `scratch/evidence/` are reproducible with `OXIDE_SMP=1 ./tools/boot-smoke.sh x86`; a live guest attached with gdb shows `#3 Spinlock::lock_irqsave<PortInner<VtConsoleDriver>,Tty> #4 TtyStruct::open_inner #6 vt_console::on_open_file #8 vfs::file::open::open_file_at #13 sys_openat`, and QMP register sampling pins RIP inside that one `cmpxchg`/`pause` loop across every sample. `make smoke-up-x86` is the gate that fails on it. | — |
| OPEN | DEFECT | CRITICAL | **Block-device lifecycle synchronization has the wrong primitive and is a P0 remediation.** The registry holds a general spinlock while admitting and releasing VFS openers, holders, and destructive lifecycle operations. The completion-side in-flight accounting is legitimately softirq-shared and must remain separately non-sleeping. Required shape: a sleepable lifecycle/open lock for publication, open/close, holder and quiesce serialization; a distinct BH-safe short admission lock/counter for in-flight I/O. | FIXED F859 | DEFECT | high | **An aarch64 `kexec_file_load(2)` relocation ran and the relocated kernel died in device probe before userspace** — a data abort on a pointer full of `0xc2` bytes inside `__insert_inode_hash` under `bdev_add` under `virtblk_probe`. Cause: this kernel's interrupt controller keeps reading its LPI configuration table and writing its pending table across the jump, and NOTHING told the next kernel. That kernel found LPIs still enabled, adopted the tables the registers pointed at, and rewrote the configuration table over its whole 64 KiB granule — while this kernel had allocated only 16 KiB (one byte per LPI at 14 ID bits), and while the range was in neither the placement map's exclusions nor the handover tree's reservations. The faulting bucket sat at `0xbf33e9a0`, `0x69a0` into the table at `0xbf338000` — past the 16 KiB allocated, inside the 64 KiB rewritten — and `0xc2` is an LPI configuration byte, which is why the value looked like poison and the victim looked unrelated. Fixed on both halves: the tables are allocated at the granule an adopting kernel assumes, and their extents are recorded once and used twice — cut from the placement map so no segment lands there, and carried into the handover tree so the new kernel's allocator never hands them out. NOTE for row below: which CPU runs the relocation decides whether the bug fires at all — the new kernel boots on that CPU's redistributor, and this kernel enables LPIs only on the boot CPU's, so a relocation from a secondary never takes the adopting path. The earlier "the parked secondary is ruled out as the differentiator" reading was wrong. | F859. `scratch/evidence/kexec-arm-file-load-userspace.log`: `kexec -s -l` rc=0, `kexec -e`, second kernel banner from `[0.000000]`, `Run /bin/sh as init process`, shell answers `NEWSH-42`; 2/2 runs reached userspace. The handover reservations are directly measured: the new kernel reports `163408K reserved` against `163280K` before, exactly the two 64 KiB tables. 11 hosted tests (`kexec::file_load::reserved`, `arm_image::handover`, `arm_image::assemble`, `arch_irq::gic_lpi_layout`, `firmware::memreserve`); positive controls — tree reservations dropped -> RED, placement subtraction disabled -> RED (incl. the end-to-end loader test), table sized at `1 << IDbits` -> RED; all restored -> GREEN. | F859 |

The rule's own rationale is about paths taken millions of times; this one is taken at most once per boot and never returns. `04§4.0` revised to carve out `klog::announce(&'static str)` for irreversible machine transitions, bounded to one name, `&'static str` only, and paths this kernel does not return from. `crates/kernel/power`'s three terminal announcements moved onto it too, so the rule is not a rule for one crate. | B2035. `klog::announce`; spec-lint `code/klog-ungated` permits that one name (positive control: swapping it for `write_raw` reports `REGRESSION crates/kernel/kexec [code/klog-ungated] 1 > 0`). | FIXED F849 | MISSING | med | x86_64 kexec now stops the machine completely before relocating. Every local-vector-table entry is masked and the local APIC software-disabled, then the boot interrupt mode is restored — APIC re-enabled on the low spurious vector, the legacy pin delivering ExtINT and the NMI pin delivering NMI — so a kernel that expects legacy delivery before it programs its own routing gets it. The I/O APIC is CLEARED rather than merely masked: an entry is masked, any level assertion still in service retired, and the entry then flattened, because a line whose remote-IRR bit stays set can never be received by the next kernel. Entries delivering system-management interrupts are left to firmware. The stop order is now interrupt sources first, then IRQs off and the other CPUs halted, then the local APIC — an interrupt delivered to an APIC mid-teardown wedges some implementations. | F849. The encoding lives in the ungated `arch_irq::lapic_shutdown` (11 host tests) precisely because the module owning the local APIC is compiled only into the kernel target, so tests written inside it never build — see the phantom-test row below. Positive controls: error entry masked last -> 1 RED; boot mode leaving the APIC software-disabled -> 1 RED; virtual-wire entry left masked -> 2 RED; the NMI pin given ExtINT delivery -> 1 RED; the version field read from the wrong byte -> 1 RED; the clean pass writing a register the implementation lacks -> 1 RED; all restored -> GREEN. | — |

(b) An MMIO debug console: on x86_64 the debug console is port-addressed and needs no mapping, but on aarch64 it is MMIO and is not in the plan. | F849. `kexec::machine::plan::ranges_for` takes RAM, segments AND firmware ranges; `pmm::setup::firmware_regions` retains them at memmap ingestion. | FIXED F849 | DEFECT | med | The aarch64 identity map's reach was a hardcoded constant that assumed one translation-control value. It is now DERIVED from the live one — the smaller of the output-address size the regime can produce and the reach the four-level 4 KiB table format describes — and the size field is PROGRAMMED for the image, break before make (regime emptied, invalidated, size written, root installed), rather than inherited. A kernel configured for a narrower output size previously got a map built for addresses its hardware truncates, with nothing able to notice. PREMISE CORRECTION on the original row: recomputing per image does not by itself reach past 2^48 here. Doing that needs the large-physical-address table format and a wider output size, which is a kernel-wide translation change, not a kexec one — this kernel is 48-bit throughout. Kept as a separate row below. | F849. `kexec::machine::plan::{arm_idmap_limit,tcr_with_idmap_t0sz,ips_bits}`, 3 host tests. Positive controls: reach assumed instead of derived -> 1 RED; one output-size encoding mis-decoded -> 1 RED; the size field ORed instead of replaced -> 1 RED; all restored -> GREEN. | — |
| FIXED F849 | DEFECT | low | The x86_64 trampoline left no descriptor table at entry, so an image that reloaded a segment register before installing its own would fault with no table to describe a handler. It now does both halves. On entry, while the running table is still live, it force-loads the kernel data selector into DS/ES/SS/FS/GS — the only thing that refreshes the hidden descriptors a segment register carries — so those descriptors hold known-good flat ring-0 segments across the window in which no table exists; nothing after that instruction may touch per-CPU state, since loading GS with a selector zeroes its base. Once the identity map is live it installs a flat four-entry table that travels inside the trampoline blob, so it lands in the control page, the one page guaranteed to sit outside every destination. | F849. `kexec::machine::plan::{GDT_ENTRY_*,GDT_LIMIT}`, 2 host tests decoding every descriptor field. Verified in the linked image, not inferred: the entry sequence disassembles to `cli` then five `mov %eax,%seg` with 0x30, and the table is 4 x 8 bytes at a 16-byte boundary inside the blob with a limit of 0x1f. Positive controls: the 64-bit entry losing its long-mode bit -> 1 RED; the data entry not writable -> 1 RED; the limit one byte too long -> 1 RED; restored -> GREEN. | — |

The reference does not depend on it; it narrows the page explicitly (`set_memory_rox`). Built the missing operation in the linear-map attribute owner and made the trampoline ASSERT the result rather than assume it, plus the symmetric restore, because a control page released while read-only is handed to the next caller with a mapping that faults on its first write, arbitrarily far from the code that narrowed it. | F845. `pmm::setup::{set_memory_rox, kernel_range_is_executable, set_memory_rw_nx}`; `kexec::machine::x86::prepare` narrows and asserts, `machine::cleanup` restores on every teardown path including the half-staged image a failing `prepare` frees. The execute decode is per-arch and falsifiable: `hal_x86_64::vmm::leaf_is_kernel_exec` (NX) and `hal_aarch64::vmm::leaf_is_kernel_exec` (PXN alone — testing UXN instead calls every kernel page non-executable). | FIXED F849 | MISSING | med | `crashkernel=` is parsed, reserved and consumed end to end, so a `KEXEC_ON_CRASH` load has somewhere legal to land and the panic path boots it. The grammar takes the plain `size[@offset]` form, range tables (`start-end:size`, open-topped, comma-chained), and the `,high` / `,low` / `,cma` suffixes, with binary suffixes and hex; placement is 16 MiB aligned, searches below the 32-bit boundary first (above it for `,high`) and falls back to the other window. The region is taken out of the page allocator before the first allocation. A crash image is staged ENTIRELY inside it: its control pages come from a bump walk over the region that steps over its own destinations, and its segments are written straight to their destinations at load time — there is nothing to relocate, and pages from the allocator would be memory the running kernel overwrites long before the panic. The panic handler tries the crash boot after the log snapshot and before the halt-or-restart branch, try-locking both the kexec lock and the slots because the panicking CPU may hold either. | F849. `kexec::crashk::{parse,place,shrink,boot,panic}` + `kexec::store::crash_kexec_now`; 55 + 5 host tests. TWO of these controls initially came back GREEN and the tests were rewritten until they could fail. | — |

The bit is live in the ABI on the platform class this kernel targets, where a load carrying it is accepted and the loaded image returns control to the kernel that started it — so the refusal is OURS. It stands only because nothing here can bring this kernel back: the contract needs the whole return path — user processes frozen, devices suspended, core system state saved on the way out and restored on the way in — and NONE of it exists (`crates/kernel/power` has no suspend path of any kind; grep for a process freeze or a device-suspend pass over the driver model returns nothing). Accepting the bit and ignoring it would turn 'resume afterwards' into 'never resume' silently, which is worse than an errno, so EINVAL is the right answer TODAY and the row stays open until the return path is built. | FIXED B2033 | MISSING | med | `IORING_SETUP_IOPOLL` was refused because "no backend here can be polled for finished I/O". That premise was TRUE and is now closed rather than argued with: `BlockDevice::poll_completions`/`can_poll` and `FileOps::iopoll`/`can_iopoll` give this kernel the reference's `f_op->iopoll` chain, virtio-blk implements it over its existing used-ring drain, and `IORING_SETUP_IOPOLL` is implemented on top — polled rings admit only the opcodes a poll can complete (EINVAL otherwise), a polled transfer must be direct and land on a pollable backend (EOPNOTSUPP otherwise), `RWF_HIPRI` is EINVAL on a ring that does not poll, and `IORING_ENTER_GETEVENTS` drives the backend poll instead of parking on a wait list nothing would signal. `HYBRID_IOPOLL` stays refused with its OWN reason now (it sleeps against a per-request service-time estimate this block layer does not keep), not "IOPOLL is refused" — see the separate row. | B2033. `io_uring_abi::iopoll` (18 hosted tests, ungated); `layout/tests.rs::every_setup_flag_is_either_implemented_or_refused` now records IOPOLL as implemented and would FAIL if it regressed to refused. Positive control: dropping the `!t.direct` clause from `admit_rw` turns 2 tests RED; restored GREEN. | — |
| FIXED F841 | DEFECT | low | A polled io_uring transfer used to run INLINE and post its completion before anything could poll for it, so `IORING_ENTER_GETEVENTS` returned from the precheck and the poll loop never ran. Closed by building the missing mechanism rather than arguing the premise: `FileOps::submit_direct` (`vfs::file_ops::DirectIo`/`DirectSubmit`) is the reference's `-EIOCBQUEUED` arm, the block bridge implements it over `BlockDevice::submit`'s existing owned-request completion, and a transfer on a polled ring is now SUBMIT-THEN-POLL: prepared in the submitter (segments imported, write payload gathered), issued to the backend, and left with NO CQE until `io_uring_enter(GETEVENTS)` drives the poll and reaps it. The request is held by a STRONG `ctx->iopoll_list` on the ring, because nothing else holds a transfer the backend owns; the backend's completion owns only the result slot, never the request or user memory, so it is safe from any context and after the ring is gone. Exactly-once completion goes through the existing `IoReq::claim`. A read's bytes are scattered into the SUBMITTER's address space by page-table root, not through whichever task reaps. | F841. Hosted: `block::devbridge::tests::a_queued_direct_read_returns_without_completing` (submitted, `slot` still empty, device holds it, only `iopoll` completes it), `a_queued_direct_write_lands_and_a_later_read_sees_it`, `f_op_submit_direct_on_an_open_block_description_reaches_the_driver`; `block::direct::plan` (9 tests). Positive control: making the fixture's `submit` complete inline turned exactly those 3 deferral tests RED; restored GREEN. | — |
| FIXED F841 | MISSING | low | `IORING_SETUP_HYBRID_IOPOLL` is implemented. The estimate it sleeps against is the ring's own running MINIMUM of observed service times — the reference's `ctx->hybrid_poll_time`, not a block-layer statistic, which is what the old refusal got wrong about where the number lives. A queued transfer is stamped at issue, each poll pass sleeps for half the current estimate before polling (once per transfer, never once per pass), and folds `now - issued_at - slept` back in as a minimum. Minimum rather than average for the reference's stated reason: with backends of different speeds, sleeping longer than the fastest takes loses completions that were ready. | F841. `io_uring_abi::iopoll::{hybrid_sleep_ns,observe_runtime,hybrid_runtime}` + 6 hosted tests, including one that feeds the estimate through its own sleep 8 times and asserts it does not ratchet. `layout/tests.rs::every_setup_flag_is_either_implemented_or_refused` now records it as implemented and fails if it regresses. | — |
| FIXED B2049 | COVERAGE | low | Closed. The reaper's SEQUENCING is now an ungated driver over a trait (`io_uring_abi::iopoll::seq::{reap_pass,poll_wait}`), and `io_uring/iopoll/queued.rs` + `io_uring/iopoll.rs` are the live adapters. The three orderings that were boot-only are pinned: claim asked LAST (a parked transfer is never claimed at all), release BEFORE post, and reap AFTER the poll that finished the transfer — including on the pass that gives up the processor, where a reap-first loop strands the completion. Driven end to end against a fixture backend that parks transfers and only finishes them on a poll, the same shape as the block layer's `QueuedDisk`. | B2049. 22 hosted tests in `io_uring_abi::iopoll::seq::tests`. Positive controls, each RED then restored GREEN: claim-before-done (2 RED), post-before-release (1 RED), reap-before-poll (2 RED), and a loop that spins on with nothing outstanding (1 RED). | — |
| FIXED F848 | MISSING | med | `SQE128` / `SQE_MIXED` / `CQE_MIXED` are implemented (`CQE32` already was). The geometry carries both strides: a dedicated wide ring doubles the array it strides, a mixed ring keeps the narrow stride and spends TWO adjacent slots on a wide entry. A mixed ring shallower than two entries is EOVERFLOW at sizing. `IORING_OP_NOP128` is dispatched, so the submission-side flags have an operation that needs them. | F848. `io_uring_abi::{sqe_slot,cqe_slot,nop}` (28 hosted tests); `sqe128_doubles_the_submission_stride_and_the_array_it_sizes`, `a_mixed_ring_keeps_the_narrow_stride_and_the_narrow_size`. Positive control: dropping the mixed wrap checks turned 4 tests RED; restored GREEN. | F848 |
| FIXED F848 | MISSING | low | `NO_MMAP` / `REGISTERED_FD_ONLY` are implemented. A `NO_MMAP` ring pins the caller's pages at `p->cq_off.user_addr`/`p->sq_off.user_addr` for its whole life and uses them in place, zeroed first; the two fields now survive admission instead of being cleared. Such a ring is not mappable from its descriptor, and `REGISTERED_FD_ONLY` installs it into the calling task's registered-ring array and returns that index instead of spending a descriptor. Per-page access is sound because no ring object straddles a page, walked over every admitted geometry. | F848. `io_uring_abi::user_ring` (4 hosted tests incl. `no_ring_object_ever_straddles_a_page`); `caller_supplied_addresses_survive_admission_and_others_are_cleared`. Positive control: dropping the alignment check and the address preservation turned 3 tests RED; restored GREEN. | F848 |
| FIXED F848 | MISSING | low | `ATTACH_WQ` is implemented: a poll thread serves a SET of rings, a ring joins the thread of a ring in its own thread group, and each ring's share of a pass is capped once more than one is attached. A descriptor naming nothing is ENXIO, a non-ring EINVAL, a ring without a thread EINVAL, a dead thread ENXIO; one naming a thread in another thread group is not refused but gets a thread of its own, because that thread borrows another process's address space. The thread now exits with its LAST ring. | F848. `io_uring_abi::sqpoll::{attach_admit,ring_take,shares,sweep}` (17 new hosted tests). Positive control: dropping the dead-thread, cross-group and disabled-ring clauses turned 3 tests RED; restored GREEN. | F848 |
| FIXED F848 | MISSING | low | `SQ_REWIND` is implemented. A rewinding ring is an array userspace rewrites in place: every pass starts at slot zero, is bounded by the array and the caller's own `to_submit` rather than by the tail, and does not publish the head it reached. The submission engine carries its own cursor for the pass instead of reading and writing the shared head word per entry. | F848. `io_uring_abi::sq_cursor` (6 hosted tests). Positive control: treating a rewinding ring as an ordinary one turned 3 tests RED; restored GREEN. | F848 |
| FIXED F848 | DEFECT | low | `IORING_SETUP_SINGLE_ISSUER` claimed the submitter lazily, at whichever `io_uring_enter`/`io_uring_register` arrived first, so a ring created by task A and first entered by task B was admitted — the case the flag exists to exclude. The submitter is now recorded at creation, or at `IORING_REGISTER_ENABLE_RINGS` for a ring created `R_DISABLED`; submission compares against the record, registration keys off whether a record exists. | F848. `io_uring_abi::issuer` (6 hosted tests). Positive control: restoring the lazy claim turned 2 tests RED; restored GREEN. | F848 |
| FIXED F848 | DEFECT | low | Two `io_uring` feature bits named behaviour this kernel HAS and were never reported, because the two constants did not exist: a wait's `min_wait_usec` batching floor, and the guarantee that a wait is not accounted as iowait (which holds vacuously — no wait here is accounted that way). A caller cannot discover a feature the kernel does not announce, so it takes the slow path it does not need. Both are now defined and reported. | F848. `features_claim_only_what_the_ring_actually_does` now walks them and asserts the bound is the full 18-bit set. | F848 |
| OPEN | MISSING | low | Two `io_uring` feature bits are deliberately NOT claimed because the behaviour is absent: send and receive consume ONE provided buffer per operation rather than a run of them, and a read or write entry carries no attribute vector. Claiming either is a lie a caller acts on, so setup reports neither. | F848. `features_claim_only_what_the_ring_actually_does` asserts both bits stay clear, and would fail if one were claimed without the work. | io_uring lane |
| FIXED B2049 | COVERAGE | low | Closed. The poll thread's LOOP — not just its per-pass decision — is now an ungated driver over an environment trait (`io_uring_abi::sqpoll::thread::poll_loop`), with `io_uring/sqpoll.rs` reduced to the live environment. 13 hosted tests cover what the `sweep` tests structurally could not see: the loop terminating on an empty ring set and on a stop, reap-before-submit within one pass, the SQ-room wake following the drain (and not happening on a reap-only pass), each ring getting its own entries, the cap not starving a neighbour, park pre-empting the pass, and a polled ring completing end to end with its submitter never entering the kernel. | B2049. `io_uring_abi::sqpoll::thread::tests`. Positive controls RED then GREEN: submit-before-reap (1 RED), a pass that never reaps a polled ring (6 RED). | — |
| FIXED B2049 | DEFECT | high | An `IORING_SETUP_SQPOLL|IORING_SETUP_IOPOLL` ring never completed anything. The poll thread drained the submission queue and handed each transfer to its backend, but nothing then polled that backend: the thread's pass had work only when entries were published, so the transfers sat outstanding forever unless the submitter entered `io_uring_enter(GETEVENTS)` by hand — which is precisely what SQPOLL exists to avoid. The reference reaps a polled ring from the poll thread itself. Fixed: a ring with outstanding transfers is work for the pass whether or not an entry is published, and the pass reaps before it submits. | B2049. `io_uring_abi::sqpoll::thread::tests::a_polled_ring_completes_without_its_submitter_entering` (three transfers, submitter never enters, all three complete). Positive control: a sweep that never sets `RingWork::reap` turns 6 tests RED. | — |
| FIXED B2049 | DEFECT | high | The same thread would then SLEEP on top of those transfers. The idle window consulted only the submission queue, so an empty one past the window published `IORING_SQ_NEED_WAKEUP` and slept — and on a polled backend nothing will ever wake it, because there is no interrupt, which is the whole point of the mode. Fixed in both places the decision is made: `step` keeps the thread hot while a served ring owes results, and `sleeps_after_arm` refuses the sleep after the doorbell is up, re-reading outstanding work across the same barrier as the tails. | B2049. `io_uring_abi::sqpoll::tests::{a_polled_ring_owing_results_forbids_the_sleep,outstanding_polled_work_keeps_the_thread_hot}` and `thread::tests::the_thread_does_not_sleep_on_outstanding_polled_work`. Positive controls: dropping either clause turns its own test RED; `an_idle_ring_does_let_the_thread_sleep` pins that the rule did not become "never sleep". | — |

| OPEN | MISSING | low | The `crashkernel=<size>,low` companion region is placed and taken out of the page allocator, but only the main region is published — nothing reads the low one, so a device that cannot address above the 32-bit boundary has no way to find its buffer area in the kernel that reads the dump. Same for `crashkernel=<size>,cma`, whose size is parsed and then discarded: no memory is lent to the page allocator and none is reclaimed on a crash. | F849. `kexec::crashk::place::Placement::{low_base,low_size}` are filled and reserved by `crashk::boot::reserve`; `crashk::publish` is called with the main region only. `CrashKernelSpec::cma` has no consumer. | — |
| OPEN | DEFECT | med | The crash region is placed against the allocator's usable-RAM ranges, which still contain the pages the kernel image, its early bookkeeping and the firmware tables occupy. A reservation that lands on one of those is silently short: the early reserve skips a page already taken and reports success, so the published region can contain memory something else owns. Mitigated but not fixed by searching top-down, which puts the region at the top of a range rather than the bottom where those users sit. | F849. `kexec::crashk::boot::reserve` feeds `pmm::setup::usable_regions()` straight to `place`; `Pmm::reserve_early` skips an already-allocated page and returns `Ok`. Needs either a reserved-range list to subtract or a `reserve_early` that reports partial success. | — |
| OPEN | COVERAGE | med | `kexec::crashk::boot::reserve` — the one side effect in the crash-reservation path — has no test that can fail. Its two inputs (the boot line and the usable ranges) and its decision (`parse_line` + `place`) are covered by 55 hosted cases, but the wiring between them, the `reserve_early` call and the `publish` are exercised only by a real boot. A reservation that silently did nothing would look identical to a machine booted without the parameter. | F849. `crashk/boot.rs` reads `pmm::setup::pmm_static()`, which is `None` hosted, so every path returns early. | — |

A fresh clone, a new worktree and CI on a clean checkout all failed to compile the crate's tests, so the vDSO cases — including the one covering a walk that indexes at offsets read out of the buffer it is walking — were checked by luck of the build directory. Fixed by a crate `build.rs` that runs the script for BOTH arches when an image is missing or older than its sources, staging through `OUT_DIR` so a concurrent compile never sees a half-written blob. A box with no aarch64 toolchain now fails the build with a message naming both accepted toolchains, never a skipped image: skipping would take the only executing check of the aarch64 restorer contract with it. `vdso/build.sh` also accepts the system GNU cross compiler (`aarch64-linux-gnu-gcc`) beside clang+LLD. | Cold worktree off `origin/main`: `cargo test --no-run -p syscalls` -> `couldn't read .../vdso-aarch64.so` , exit 101. ### B1719-procfs-per-superblock-root

| Status | Class | Sev | Issue | Evidence | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| OPEN | COVERAGE | med | **Nothing in `procfs` can be tested through an actual mount.** `fs_impl`, `static_files` and `live` are all `#[cfg(target_os = "oxide-kernel")]`, so `ProcfsFs::new` / `build_root` / the lookup and readdir enforcement have no hosted reach — a test written against them is a phantom, which is why this lane's mount test was deleted rather than shipped. Every DECISION is ungated and tested (`fs_info`, 16 tests); the WIRING is proven only by the guest probe recorded above. Closing it needs the root-building split so an inode tree can be built hosted, the way `tmpfs` already can be. | `cargo test -p procfs` builds none of `fs_impl`; the deleted `tests/mount_identity.rs` failed to compile with `unresolved import procfs::fs_impl`. | unowned |

### B1720-fsmount-real-vfsmount

| Status | Class | Sev | Issue | Evidence | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| OPEN | INFRA | med | **A `make feature-gate` can report GREEN for the wrong tree.** `cd <worktree> && (gate) & (smoke) & wait` parses as `{ cd && gate } &` then `{ smoke } &` — `&` binds looser than `&&` — so the second job runs in the shell's original directory, normally the MAIN tree. Observed here: the gate type-checked `main` and passed while this branch's kernel build was broken by a missing re-export (`E0425`), which only `make x86` caught. Not a defect in the gate itself (a correctly-targeted run catches it: exit=2 with the error) but a foot-gun that the newly-mandated parallel-checks rule makes MORE likely. `CLAUDE.md` now carries the shape and says to confirm the gate log names your worktree. A `make` guard that refuses to run when `$(CURDIR)` is not the git worktree root of the checked source would remove it entirely. | This lane, twice: gate-exit=0 with `Checking vfs (/home/nd/oxide/kernel/...)` in the log while the worktree was `/home/nd/oxide/kernel-B1720`; after the fix, gate-exit=0 with the worktree's own paths. | unowned |

### B1722-cgroup2-mount-options

| Status | Class | Sev | Issue | Evidence | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| OPEN | MISSING | med | **Four cgroup2 flags are accepted and stored but cannot yet be acted on, because the mechanism they gate does not exist here.** They are declared because omitting a name fails a mount the reference accepts — a container runtime passing `memory_recursiveprot` would not mount at all — but no code reads them yet: (1) `memory_recursiveprot`: `memory.min`/`memory.low` are stored on the node and read by NOTHING; there is no effective-protection computation of any kind, recursive or not, so there is no behaviour to switch. (2) `pids_localevents`: `pids.events` is the hardcoded literal `"max 0\n"` — no pids-max-breach counter exists to be local or hierarchical. (3) `memory_hugetlb_accounting`: no hugetlb exists in this kernel at all. (4) `favordynmods`: a locking-strategy choice with no user-visible semantics and no dynamic-modification cost model here. Each becomes enforceable when its controller does; `RootFlag`'s own doc comments say which are enforced today. | `crates/kernel/cgroup/src/root_flags.rs` `RootFlag`; `tree/files.rs:44` (`"pids.events" => "max 0\n"`); `mem_low`/`mem_min` have no reader outside their own read/write handlers. | unowned |
| OPEN | COVERAGE | med | **The cgroup2 flag path has no in-guest proof.** The boot mounts `/sys/fs/cgroup` from the kernel with no options and this image's systemd passes none, so a normal boot exercises only the default. ### B1730-irq-nesting-unbounded

| Status | Class | Sev | Issue | Evidence | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| OPEN | MISSING | med | **No interrupt-nesting depth bound and no runtime stack high-water mark.** Nesting is now structurally bounded at two levels, but nothing measures or enforces it, so a future change that reopens a window fails the same silent way. The reference reports a stack's deepest use (`CONFIG_DEBUG_STACK_USAGE`: zero-fill at allocation — which `kstack::alloc` already does — then scan upward from the low end to the first non-zero word, reported at task exit by `check_stack_usage`). Adding that plus an explicit nesting counter would make a regression measurable instead of fatal. Design worked out this lane: scan in `Drop for Task`, one global low-water atomic, and an on-demand sysrq key for the interrupt stack, which is never freed and so has no exit point. | Reference: `kernel/exit.c` `stack_not_used`/`check_stack_usage`; `kernel/fork.c` `GFP_VMAP_STACK` zero-fill. Our zero-fill is already at `kstack.rs` `alloc`. | unowned |

### B1731-failed-units-boot-health

| Status | Class | Sev | Issue | Evidence | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| OPEN | DEFECT | med | **`systemctl` intermittently cannot reach the service manager: `Failed to list units: Transport endpoint is not connected`.** Seen once ~75 s into a live-gnome boot, from a shell that had worked moments earlier; a later run at ~40 s queried cleanly. ENOTCONN on the manager's AF_UNIX socket means the peer went away or the connection was reset, which on a healthy system does not happen. Recorded because it silently turns a verification query into an empty result — an empty `systemctl --failed` was briefly mistaken for "nothing fails". Any probe that reads unit state must check for this line before believing the answer. | `verify2.log`: `Failed to list units: Transport endpoint is not connected`, with the same command succeeding in `verify3.log`. | unowned |
| OPEN | INFRA | med | **Boot probes were used to answer questions the source answers.** Roughly eight boots went into this lane, several of them to establish facts a grep would have given in seconds, and two produced no usable answer at all because the typed probe was mangled by interleaved kernel output or swallowed by `systemctl`'s pager. The repo's own rule says a harness beats a boot and that more than two boots chasing one bug means the loop is the bug. Practical notes for the next lane: pass `SYSTEMD_PAGER=cat` and `--no-pager` or the probe hangs in `less`; the alive-marker must not appear in the typed command (use the quote-split form); and `rc=$?` after a pipeline reports the LAST command's status, not the one being tested — that mistake produced a false `rc=0` for three mounts here. | This lane. | unowned |

### D513-real-hardware-findings

| Status | Class | Sev | Issue | Evidence | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| IN-PROGRESS B2111-xhci-linux-audit | MISSING | blocker | **Native PCI xHCI now exists and is registered before PCI driver probing:** class `0x0c0330`, IOMMU-mapped DMA pages, controller reset/run, MSI/MSI-X binding, root-port address/configure, USB HID boot keyboard/mouse reports, platform-keyed evdev publication, and root-port connect/disconnect handling through the USB softirq. The old claim that no xHCI or bus-agnostic input existed is false. USB mass-storage descriptor discovery, Bulk-Only/SCSI CBW/CSW contracts, generic bulk endpoint-context construction, retained requester-keyed bulk DMA owners, non-overwriting transfer-completion handoff, native configuration sequencing, native CBW/data/CSW transfer submission, initial INQUIRY/READ CAPACITY transaction sequencing, canonical `sd*` block-device publication, and page-vector payload transfers now exist. One transaction can map up to 255 IOMMU DMA pages and submits chained Normal TRBs with a completion only on the terminal buffer. Root addressing now flows through an xHCI topology owner that preserves a physical root port plus route string. Hub-class status endpoints are parsed, configured, submitted, completion-validated, DMA-invalidated, and rearmed through that same transport owner; the hub descriptor is fetched in two stages, then its protocol/TT facts update the xHCI hub slot context. Hub initialization powers every downstream port, waits its one descriptor-provided power-good interval, reads changed/initial port status in a process worker, acknowledges change features, resets connected ports, and publishes descendant slots with the parent route string. The port reset-control transaction drops controller/device locks before completion waits and recovery delay. Descendant detach and recursive topology are still absent. The adapter owns the SCSI disk reservation until detach, rejects reuse when the canonical registry cannot detach an open disk, and serializes every command through the controller-then-device transaction gate. The shared block registry owns the reusable SCSI `sd*` identity allocator, so SATA and USB storage do not publish through competing counters; a shared SCSI midlayer, full hub topology, HID report-descriptor support beyond boot protocol, and physical xHCI exercise remain. The Linux module facade remains a shim and is not the native-driver path. | `drv-xhci::{probe,probe_hub,storage_block,irq,completion,usb,storage,context,hid,device}`; `block::{registry,BlockDevice}`; `pci-boot::register_pci_model_drivers`; `input::{InputDeviceKey::platform,install,publish_evdev}`; `irq::take_port_changes` → `probe::service_port_changes`. | B2111-xhci-linux-audit; B2112-usb-mass-storage; B211301-xhci-bulk-endpoints; B2113-xhci-storage-dma; B2114-xhci-completion-queue; B2115-xhci-storage-probe; B2116-xhci-bot-execution; B2118-block-sd-name-allocator; B2119-xhci-controller-ownership; B2120-xhci-controller-handle; B2121-xhci-storage-transaction; B2122-xhci-storage-block-device; B2124-xhci-storage-scatter-gather; B2125-xhci-hub-topology; B2126-xhci-hub-endpoint; B2127-xhci-hub-port-control; B2128-xhci-hub-port-status; B2129-xhci-hub-child-enumeration |

Remaining x2APIC prerequisites are HPET remapping and scalar CPU-targeting migrations. | `firmware::{ioapic_id,acpi}`; `iommu::{vtd,vtd_ir,vtd_manager}`; `arch-irq::program_x86_ioapic`; `hal-x86_64::ioapic::program_remapped_redirect` | B2100-vtd-ioapic-interrupt-remapping |
| IN-PROGRESS B210101-vtd-hpet-interrupt-remapping | MISSING | med | **HPET firmware identity and VT-d source ownership are now retained as one contract.** The ACPI HPET decoder publishes its MMIO base and block number; VT-d resolves that block number through the DMAR HPET scope, allocates an HPET-qualified IRTE, and returns a remapped FSB message only after selective invalidation. A published HPET with no trustworthy scope is refused. Remaining closure is a real HPET clockevent owner using this message path instead of the current LAPIC-only timer path. | `firmware::{hpet_pa,hpet_id}`; `iommu::{intel_vtd_hpet_source,allocate_vtd_hpet,VtdIrte::hpet}`; `smoke/src/device_map/x86.rs` | B210101-vtd-hpet-interrupt-remapping |

Remaining scalar target consumers are tracked in the B2098 row and must move through the same mask-shaped owner before x2APIC is enabled. | `sched/src/membarrier/work.rs`; `cpu/src/mask.rs`; `mm-vmm/src/address_space.rs::cpumask_full` | B2101-x2apic-cpu-targeting |

B2097 adds VT-d's IRTE/IRTA/IRE path and routes PCI MSI/MSI-X through it when the owning unit supports it. B2098 now preserves DMAR's x2APIC opt-out policy and rejects EIM unless every published DRHD advertises queued invalidation, interrupt remapping, and EIM — the same all-IOMMU decision Linux makes. What remains reachable from ptrace is `signal_common::{read,write}_user_siginfo`, which GETSIGINFO/SETSIGINFO/PEEKSIGINFO call — boundary (3)'s; (3) ~60 hits spread over the remaining `syscalls` slot files and helpers (`iov`, `signal_common`, `clock_ops`, `aio/*`, `siocgif`, `statmount_target`, `tcp_info`, `mount_common`, `netlink_fd`, `fcntl_lease`, `pselect_ppoll_edge`); **(4) `fs` — CLOSED by B2054, 21 hits converted** (`epoll/fileops` 8, `epoll/syscalls` 5, `epoll/scan` 2, `fileattr` 3, `signalfd/syscalls` 1, `userfaultfd/ioctl/{structs,api,register}` 4+1, `watch_queue/ioctl` 1, `xattr/user` 2, plus `userfaultfd/work/install::fill_frame`); **(5) `ipc` — CLOSED by B2054, 16 hits converted** (`live/futex/core` 2, `live/futex/robust` 1, `live/posix_mq/user` 6, `live/posix_mq/wait` 2, `sysv_shm/shmctl` 4, `sysv/user` 2); (6) `sched` + `security/seccomp/user` + `syscall::numa` — **CLOSED by B2055** (22 hits: `cred/caps.rs` 9, `prctl/name.rs` 2, `prctl_set_mm.rs` 2, `security/seccomp/user.rs` 7, plus 7 more found in the same crate during the re-sweep, `sched/src/xfer.rs` `sendfile`'s `loff_t *offset` — not in the D552 inventory, and its range check covered only the FIRST byte of the 8. | OPEN | DEFECT | high | **`cmpxchg_user_u32` performs an atomic read-modify-write DIRECTLY on a user address with no exception-table fixup**, so the PI-futex and robust-futex paths still fault the kernel when the word goes away — the one remaining raw user dereference in `ipc` after B2054. It is reached from `futex/pi/{lock,unlock,requeue,exit}.rs` and `futex/robust.rs`, each of which guards it with a `user_addr_accessible(uaddr, true)` page-table probe. The probe does not close the window: the futex word belongs to a live multi-threaded process whose PEERS can unmap it between the probe and the RMW, and on the robust/PI exit paths the guard is all that stands between a hostile userspace and a kernel fault. The reference has a dedicated fixup-carrying primitive for exactly this (`futex_atomic_cmpxchg_inatomic`), which returns `-EFAULT` instead of faulting. The fix is an arch asm routine per HAL — `lock cmpxchg` with an `__ex_table` entry on x86_64, `ldaxr/stlxr` with one on aarch64 — surfaced through `uaccess` beside `raw_copy_{from,to}_user`, after which every `user_addr_accessible(_, true)` call in `ipc` can go. | `crates/kernel/ipc/src/live/futex/core.rs::cmpxchg_user_u32` builds an `&AtomicU32` straight from the user VA; `crates/arch/hal-{x86_64,aarch64}/src/uaccess.rs` contains only the two byte-copy routines, so nothing else in the tree has a fixup. | OPEN | DEFECT | med | **`uaccess` has no single-instruction `get_user`/`put_user`, so every scalar user access in the kernel pays a byte-copy routine — and on aarch64 that routine copies ONE BYTE PER ITERATION.** `oxide_raw_copy_from_user`/`_to_user` are `rep movsb` on x86_64 (acceptable for a 4-byte fetch, ~an order of magnitude over a single `mov`) and a `ldrb`/`strb` loop with a `subs`/`b.ne` on aarch64, which is byte-rate for EVERY copy in the kernel, not only the scalar ones — a `read(2)` of a page moves 4096 bytes one at a time. The reference has both: `__get_user`/`__put_user` are a single fixup-carrying load or store, and `copy_from_user` is a tuned bulk routine. Two separate wins: a `get_user_u32`/`put_user_u32`/`get_user_u64` family for the hot scalar paths (`futex_get_value_locked` is on FUTEX_WAIT, the hottest lock path in the system), and a word-at-a-time aarch64 bulk copy. | `crates/arch/hal-aarch64/src/uaccess.rs` — the whole body is `1: ldrb w3,[x1],#1; 2: strb w3,[x0],#1; subs x2,x2,#1; b.ne 1b`. B2054 converted `ipc`'s futex fetches to `copy_from_user` on a 4-byte stack array rather than add arch asm outside its lane; no bounce buffer is allocated, but the call is still the byte loop on ARM. | unassigned |

| OPEN | DEFECT | med | Process-group and session ids are NOT stored untranslated — they are stamped in vpid space at spawn/fork and stay self-consistent inside one pid namespace. Closing it means giving the thread group a pid REFERENCE rather than a number — the translation machinery already exists (`registry::vpid::group_chain`, which `/proc/<pid>/status` `NSpgid`/`NSsid` already uses) — and then converting every reader at once: a half-converted set of readers is exactly the split source of truth this project bans. | FIXED B2034 | COVERAGE | med | Rows 61/247: no hosted harness driving `sys_wait4` / `sys_waitid` themselves. ### B1979-gnome-segfault-regression-triage

| Status | Class | Sev | Issue | Evidence | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| OPEN | DEFECT | High | A process that loads the GIO module `libdconfsettings.so` intermittently dies with `SIGSEGV` on the FIRST read of the dconf database it has just mapped. The faulting instruction is `mov (%rax),%edx` at module offset `0x5245`, where `%rax` is the pointer `g_mapped_file_new()` → `g_mapped_file_get_bytes()` → `g_bytes_get_data()` just handed back and the size check `cmpq $0x17,0x10(%rbx)` has already passed — so userspace holds a mapping the kernel then refuses to fault in. When the victim is `gnome-shell` the whole desktop dies to a bare console; when it is a session service (`evolution-source-registry`) the desktop survives. **Not introduced by the 2026-08-08 wave** — it reproduces on `5506b785e`, the last-known-good commit, at the SAME module offset. | Paired live-gnome boots, same host, same qemu invocation, run concurrently: `910470808` 0 faults / 5 boots, `5506b785e` 1 fault / 5 boots (plus one earlier `910470808` boot under a wider device set, which faulted — so 1/6 vs 1/5, indistinguishable at this sample size). Both fault lines carry `ino 0x6e54000000003e14` (ext4 inode 15892 = `/usr/lib64/gio/modules/libdconfsettings.so`) and `off 0x5245`; only the victim process and the ASLR bases differ. Screendump of the `5506b785e` boot is a black console holding the `gnome-shell` segfault line; every non-faulting boot on BOTH commits reaches the GNOME overview. Logs: `main-1`, `maina-1..5`, `basea-1..5` under the B1979 scratch dir. | unowned |

### B1985-fault-fill-failure-reports-sigbus

| Status | Class | Sev | Issue | Evidence | Owner |

| OPEN | MISSING | med | **There is no global out-of-memory killer, and the fault path's OOM leg now depends on one.** A fault that cannot obtain a frame reports the reference's out-of-memory outcome: no signal, resume userspace, re-take the instruction — on the contract that the memory-pressure path has already posted a fatal signal on its victim. That contract holds for a memcg charge failure (`mm-pmm/src/memcg.rs` `MemoryPressure::Max` reclaims, then `sched::oom::kill_memcg`), and does NOT hold for plain frame exhaustion: `alloc_one_frame` returning `None` kills nothing, so the instruction re-faults forever. The reference relies on its page allocator invoking the OOM killer before returning failure, which is why its own fault-side handler only warns and retries. Closing this means a global OOM selector on the allocator failure path, not a change to the fault classification. | `crates/kernel/mm-vmm/src/fault_signal.rs` `FaultFailure::Oom`; the only OOM selector in the tree is `sched::oom::kill_memcg`, reached solely from the cgroup pressure hook (grep: no other caller). Partly mitigated: `mm-pmm/src/user_as/signal.rs` prints `[FAULT-RESOLVE] out of memory; no signal, re-taking` once per boot, but only under `debug-faultdiag` — the klog policy (`04§4.0`, spec-lint `code/klog-ungated`) forbids an ungated production log, so a default build hangs silently. Previously this reason terminated the task with SIGSEGV, which is not what the reference reports. | unowned |

### B1986-main-aarch64-stack-overrun

| Status | Class | Sev | Issue | Evidence | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| OPEN | DEFECT | high | **The socket destructor runs inline on whoever drops the last reference, so a 4,544 B teardown cascade is charged to every path in the kernel that can touch a socket.** (D551: `stack-gate` is GREEN on both arches as of 44765839c, so this is no longer a failing gate — it is a deep path with ~4 KB of headroom left, and the gate will fail on the next thing that lands on top of it.) `Drop for InetSocket` calls `release_file`, and from there: `release_file` 352 -> `tcp_fastopen::drain_client` 624 -> `net_ns::state::materialize_state` 1,168 -> `NsNet` / `UnixListener` / `UnixPair` drops -> `Arc<Task>` -> FdTable -> `filp_close` -> `File` -> mount -> `MntNamespace` -> `generic_shutdown_super` -> writeback. The reference does not do this: `__sk_free` hands `__sk_destruct` to `call_rcu`, so the destructor runs from an RCU callback in process context. **RETRACTION (B1996): the `in_interrupt()` branch in `release_file` is NOT a split truth and must not be "unified" away.** It is the reference's own distinction between close-time protocol shutdown (`inet_release`: process context, synchronous, takes RTNL) and final destruction from an arbitrary context (`__sk_destruct`: deferred). Making the deferral unconditional was tried and is WRONG — it breaks the contract that a close releases its device references immediately, and seven hosted tests assert exactly that (`final_close_flushes_every_unique_device_reference`, `process_context_final_drop_still_releases_inline`, `socket_anycast_is_not_multicast_and_close_releases_device_ref`, and four more). Closing this row is therefore NOT about when the destructor runs. It is about making the destructor path SHALLOW: the deep frames under it (`tcp_fastopen::drain_client` 624, `materialize_state` 1,168) have to shrink, or the owned state has to be moved out of the socket into a queued bundle so the drop itself is a hand-off. ### B1987-oom-fault-leg-livelocks

| Status | Class | Sev | Issue | Evidence | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| OPEN | MISSING | med | The allocation slowpath bounds the number of out-of-memory selector invocations one allocation may make (`mm-pmm::oom_entry::MAX_OOM_ATTEMPTS`, 16) and then reports `NoMem`. The reference has no such bound — it retries for as long as the killer reports progress — which is safe there only because a reaper frees the victim's memory asynchronously, so "progress" becomes free pages within a bounded time. Without a reaper an unbounded loop here would spin against a victim that will not die, so the bound is a stand-in for the reaper and must be removed once one exists. | `mm-pmm/src/oom_entry.rs::after_oom`; `oom_entry::tests::kill_invocations_are_bounded_per_allocation` pins the current behaviour so its removal is visible | unassigned |
| OPEN | MISSING | HIGH | No OOM reaper and no give-up on a victim that will not die. The "a victim is already exiting, kill nothing more" rule has no escape hatch: a victim wedged in an uninterruptible sleep leaves the scan reporting in-progress forever, so a later exhaustion can never select anyone and the fault leg retakes indefinitely. The reference reaps the victim's mm asynchronously and, when that fails, marks the mm skippable so selection resumes. | `oom::select::select_victim` returns `InProgress` for as long as `Task::oom_victim` is set and the task is alive; `oom::tests::the_scope_resumes_selecting_once_the_victim_has_gone` shows selection only resumes when the victim actually reaches zombie | unassigned |
| OPEN | MISSING | MED | No coredump-in-progress state on the thread group, so the "already dying" shortcut cannot exclude a process that is dumping core. The reference refuses to treat a coredumping process as one that will promptly free memory, because the dump can sleep for a long time; here such a process short-circuits the whole selection to `SelfWillFree` and nothing is killed. | `sched::oom::kill::will_free_mem` checks group-exit latch + pending SIGKILL only; no `core_state` equivalent exists anywhere in `crates/kernel/sched/src` | unassigned |
| OPEN | DEFECT | LOW | An OOM kill is not reported on a production console. The reference prints the victim unconditionally; `04§4.0` is frozen on every `klog` call site being `cfg`-elidable, so the report sits behind `debug-sched`. The kill is still counted (`/proc/vmstat` `oom_kill`, victim cgroup `memory.events` `oom_kill`), so it is observable — but a service that vanished under pressure leaves no console record. | `sched/src/oom/kill.rs::report` under `#[cfg(all(target_os = "oxide-kernel", feature = "debug-sched"))]`; the ungated form regresses the `crates/kernel/sched [code/klog-ungated]` ratchet key 163 → 168 | unassigned |

The ACK wait and the single-in-flight `OWNER` handoff are equally unpinned. LANE SCOPE: model the sender/target pair under loom (the repo already uses loom elsewhere) — one sender publishing KIND+PENDING against one or more targets running `service()`, asserting that any target which clears its bit observed the round's own KIND. | OPEN | DEFECT | MED | ext4 drops the `datasync` argument: `Ext4RegFileOps::fsync` and `Ext4StatFileOps::fsync` both take `_datasync` and discard it, so `fdatasync(2)` always pays a full mount-wide metadata commit plus an unconditional device barrier. The reference waits only on the inode's `i_datasync_tid` and skips the commit entirely when that transaction already committed, and issues the barrier only when its own commit is the one owing it. Three linked divergences: datasync ignored; the commit drains the whole mount's shadow map rather than this inode's transaction; the barrier is unconditional (a 4th flush on top of the 3 the WAL already issues) and ignores `nobarrier`. NOTE: gating the barrier on "did the commit write anything" is NOT a valid fix — it is only accidentally safe because `write_at_inner` unconditionally restages the inode block, so any future skip of that restage, or any data path not going through `write_at`, silently loses durability. The Linux-shaped fix needs a per-mount commit generation plus per-inode `i_sync_tid`/`i_datasync_tid` and a barrier watermark. | `crates/kernel/ext4/src/rootfs/inode/regular.rs:241`, `special.rs:284`, `ext4_sync_file` at `regular.rs:28-35`, `mount/batch.rs::commit_batch`. The owner is the SAME CPU's own stack: the VT write path holds `inner` (irqsave) across the fbcon render, whose `lock_vt()` (`lock_bh`) guard drop ran `local_bh_enable` -> inline softirq DRAIN inside the irqsave section; a drained handler (InputDrain delivering to this same VT via `receive_from_driver`) re-takes `inner` and self-deadlocks with no tick to break it. The reference forbids exactly this (`local_bh_enable` with IRQs off is a lockdep assertion). Fixed at the mechanism: `local_bh_enable` refuses the inline drain while local IRQs are masked (`bh_enable_may_drain`, `preempt::irqs_disabled()` promoted ungated); pending work runs at the next IRQ exit or ksoftirqd pass, as it already does for the hard-IRQ case. Survived B2007/B2008/B2009 because it is the INVERSE shape: not a bare lock in process context, but a bh-guard release inside an irqsave section — invisible to the `needs lock_bh` lockdep report (that boot was clean). | GDB attach on the frozen guest: RIP in `Spinlock::lock_irqsave<PortInner<VtConsoleDriver>>` from `TtyStruct::open` <- `vt_console on_open_file` <- `sys_openat`; serial frozen at [7.795] "Started debug-shell", 97% CPU, sysrq dead. Hosted: `bh::tests::no_drain_while_irqs_are_masked` goes red when the `!irqs_off` conjunct is removed. | B2010 |

| OPEN | DEFECT | low | An io_uring region is one CONTIGUOUS buddy run; the reference allocates it as an unordered page vector reached through a kernel virtual mapping. Contiguity is forced by `VmaBacking::KernelFrame`, which names a region by its first PA; a page-vector backing in `mm-vmm` would remove both costs. | FIXED F850 | MISSING | MED | **An `SK_REUSEPORT` program can now NAME a member.** `BPF_MAP_TYPE_REUSEPORT_SOCKARRAY` and `bpf_sk_select_reuseport` (helper 82) both exist and are wired: a program stores socket descriptors into the map from userspace, names a slot at run time, and the named socket becomes the index the arriving packet is handed to. | OPEN | MISSING | HIGH | Standard atomic connector-state reset/duplicate/destroy now owns its CRTC-commit reference as well as the connector reference for the externally used null-resource state, but complete state ownership still needs `drm_property_blob_get/put` and `drm_writeback_cleanup_job`.
