| OPEN | MISSING | HIGH | Standard CRTC atomic state now releases its CRTC-commit reference and preserves Linux's transient-field clearing, but blob ownership remains absent. A state carrying mode/color blobs is refused rather than copied or released incorrectly. | F1101 `linux_drm_crtc_commit`; `linux_drm_atomic_crtc::tests::standard_crtc_states_reset_duplicate_and_clear_transient_fields` exercises state destruction with an owned commit. | B2133-linux-drm-kmi |
| OPEN | MISSING | MED | `drm_edid_read_custom` now matches Linux block acquisition (retry, header repair, CTA checksum exception, HF-EEODB expansion, and invalid-extension filtering), but Oxide has no `drm_edid_override_get` equivalent: debugfs/firmware EDID overrides, `connector->edid_corrupt`, bad-EDID counters/checksum accounting, and their connector-field ABI must be implemented before the complete Linux read path is present. | `linux_drm_edid_read::drm_edid_read_custom` deliberately has no connector-field access; compare `../linux-master/drivers/gpu/drm/drm_edid.c::_drm_do_get_edid`. | B2133-linux-drm-kmi |
| OPEN | MISSING | HIGH | `drm_edid_connector_update` owns and replaces the BTF-verified connector EDID blob and `drm_edid_connector_add_modes` publishes valid base-block detailed timings, but it does not yet implement Linux's CTA/DisplayID detailed timings, CEA VICs, CVT, standard/established timings, inferred GTF/CVT modes, display-info/ELD/HDR/tile/non-desktop properties, or global DRM property ownership. Treating base DTD publication as a complete EDID/KMS parser would leave physical displays with incomplete mode sets. | `linux_drm_edid_connector`; compare `../linux-master/drivers/gpu/drm/drm_edid.c::_drm_edid_connector_add_modes` and `_drm_edid_connector_property_update`. | B2133-linux-drm-kmi |
| OPEN | MISSING | HIGH | Generic `drm_object_attach_property` now uses Linux's BTF-verified 64-slot object owner; mode-config owns the standard `EDID` and `FB_DAMAGE_CLIPS` blob properties and their connector/plane helpers. The remaining standard atomic CRTC/plane properties, generic driver-created property constructors/destructors, property ioctls, blob reference ownership, and atomic property callbacks/state routing are still absent. | `linux_drm_properties`; compare `../linux-master/drivers/gpu/drm/drm_property.c`, `drm_mode_config.c`, and `drm_mode_object.c`. | B2133-linux-drm-kmi |
| IN-PROGRESS B2133 | MISSING | HIGH | Client initialization owns modeset storage, links both the ABI client node and its private file into the canonical device lists, and device unregister invokes the unregister-or-release path. `drm_client_setup` now allocates, prepares, registers, and tears down the standard fbdev client using the canonical device helper slot. The remaining blocker is complete `drm_fb_helper` initial configuration plus `drm_fbdev_shmem` buffer/probe/framebuffer publication, without which an external DRM driver cannot expose an emulated fbdev node. | `linux_drm_fbdev_client::client_setup_allocates_registers_and_releases_one_fbdev_client`; external Bochs KPI audit. | B2133-linux-drm-kmi |
| OPEN | MISSING | LOW | `__drm_dev_dbg` has the exact external variadic ABI but follows Linux's default-disabled debug gate until Oxide implements the DRM debug-category mask and its module/debugfs control plane; external driver debug records are therefore suppressed rather than selectively emitted. | `linux_drm_print::drm_dev_dbg`; compare `../linux-master/drivers/gpu/drm/drm_print.c::__drm_dev_dbg` and `_drm_debug_enabled(category)`. | B2133-linux-drm-kmi |
| FIXED B2175 | DEFECT | CRITICAL | Atomic vblank flush acquired the event queue lock before taking its vblank/device reference, while timer delivery held the device registry lock before acquiring the event queue lock. Flush now acquires the vblank reference before entering the event queue, so both paths use device-registry then event-queue order and cannot form an ABBA deadlock. | `linux_drm_vblank_event::tests::atomic_flush_queues_a_live_vblank_event`; modules hosted suite. | B2175-drm-vblank-event-lock-order |
| FIXED F984 | MISSING | HIGH | External KMS drivers had no DRM-core dispatch for standard dumb-buffer create/destroy and no file-authorized GEM fake mmap offset owner. The core now clears create outputs before invoking the registered driver callback, destroys through the same file-owned handle authority, and gives each local GEM object one stable page-aligned offset with exact file and range lookup. VMA installation, close ownership, and shmem page mapping remain separate open work; an offset is not treated as a successful mapping. | `linux_drm_ioctl::tests::standard_dumb_create_clears_outputs_then_calls_the_driver`; `linux_drm_gem::tests::dumb_map_offset_is_file_authorized_stable_and_page_aligned`; `cargo test -p modules --lib -- --test-threads=1` (330 passed). | F984-linux-driver-mmap-vma |
| FIXED F985 | MISSING | HIGH | The exported GEM mmap entry points had no VMA/object lifetime contract. `drm_gem_mmap` now resolves only the exact authorized fake offset and takes a temporary lookup reference; `drm_gem_mmap_obj` verifies range, takes the VMA-owned reference, binds `vm_private_data` and `vm_ops`, invokes object mmap where supplied or installs the standard non-expandable PFN mapping flags, and the shmem VM close drops that reference exactly once. F987 moves shmem dumb storage to the canonical PMM-backed vmalloc owner, preserving its CPU-visible VA while retaining exact physical pages for the VMA fault path. F988 gives every file-backed mapping one pre-publication VMM callback with its final address range and page offset; F989 routes mmap through the character driver retained by that exact open file. F990 invokes an external `f_op->mmap` through that path, accepts only its verified shmem-GEM result, maps the exact PMM page on shared faults, and owns one GEM reference per VMA birth/close. Other external mmap implementations remain refused until they provide their own persistent frame model. | `linux_drm_gem_mmap::tests::gem_mmap_binds_the_exact_authorized_object_to_the_vma`; modules hosted suite. | F985-drm-gem-vma-lifecycle |
| FIXED F986 | DEFECT | CRITICAL | The character-device shared-frame fault probe called every external `file_operations.mmap` callback with a null VMA and discarded its result. A normal Linux mmap callback dereferences the VMA, so this could fault or corrupt module state during an unrelated page fault while never creating a mapping. The probe now returns no frame without invoking a callback; only the syscall mmap owner may construct the persistent VMA and call the driver. | `linux_chrdev::core::tests::device_node_routes_open_state_poll_mmap_and_release` proves the probe leaves the callback count at zero; modules hosted suite. | F986-linux-char-mmap-context |
| IN-PROGRESS B2174 | MISSING | HIGH | **Native PCI graphics has a QEMU std-VGA/Bochs model driver and a DRM/KMS dumb-buffer presentation path, but generic physical GPU coverage and external Linux DRM driver loading remain incomplete.** The native driver claims the PCI aperture, registers a DRM card, and presents validated XRGB/ARGB dumb buffers through the linear framebuffer owner; the remaining Linux DRM KPI deficit must be closed before external Bochs and wider drivers can load. F995 supplies atomic validation, B2183 atomically publishes state ownership, and F1102 installs the CRTC/connector/plane commit references needed to sequence later work. The generic commit tail and legacy bridge paths remain required. | `linux_drm::atomic_commit_setup::tests::setup_gives_an_inactive_crtc_state_and_entry_retained_commit_references`; host Bochs audit still has 9 unresolved imports. | B2174-native-pci-display-coverage |
| FIXED B2179 | DEFECT | HIGH | DRM atomic modeset checking had two incompatible symbol providers: the later registration happened to overwrite the earlier one. The public symbol now has one owner, so routing, affected-object expansion, callback validation, and mode fixup cannot be replaced by registration order. | `linux_drm::tests::modeset_helper_has_one_canonical_export_owner`; the positive control replaces the symbol with the plane-check address and the test fails. | B2179-drm-modeset-export-owner |
| FIXED B2169 | DEFECT | CRITICAL | The module character-device and debugfs adapters represented `file_operations`, `file`, and `inode` as compact local structs, so a normal external module's callbacks and callback arguments used invalid offsets. The adapters now use the verified object sizes and callback/private-data offsets; both internal adapters construct those ABI-shaped objects. | `modules::linux_chrdev::core::tests::external_file_callback_abi_uses_the_linux_object_offsets`; `cargo test -p modules --lib -- --nocapture --test-threads=1` (284 passed). | B2169-linux-file-operations-abi |
| FIXED B2134 | DEFECT | HIGH | A driver-private DRM allocation could be freed while its driver was inside a device critical section. The DRM module owner now issues a unique enter token, refuses new entries after release begins, and defers the final free until the matching final exit. | `modules::linux_drm` tests cover token release and put-during-active-section ordering. | B2134-drm-device-guards |
| FIXED B2135 | DEFECT | HIGH | DRM hot-unplug had no synchronized transition, allowing new driver sections to enter while teardown began. `drm_dev_unplug` now marks the device unavailable, waits with the shared wait-loop until prior entries drain, and is woken only by matching exits. | `modules::linux_drm` tests cover unplug refusal and exported entry points. | B2135-drm-unplug |
| FIXED B2138 | DEFECT | CRITICAL | `__devm_drm_dev_alloc` returned the embedded DRM-device address rather than the driver container, so any external driver deriving its private state from the allocation corrupted its own layout before registration. It now returns the container, initializes the embedded device's parent, DMA parent, driver, driver-feature copy, final allocation pointer, and initial reference, exports `drm_dev_get`, and binds the initial put to parent devres. | `modules::linux_drm::tests::allocation_returns_the_container_and_initializes_the_embedded_device`; 7 DRM lifetime tests. | B2138-drm-device-registration |
| FIXED B2139 | MISSING | HIGH | External KMS drivers had no mode-config initializer, leaving every plane/CRTC/connector/object list uninitialized. `drmm_mode_config_init` now initializes the embedded KMS object lists in the managed device, records one-shot ownership, and refuses invalid or duplicate initialization. | `modules::linux_drm::tests::mode_config_initializes_each_object_list_once`. | B2139-drm-mode-config |
| FIXED B2140 | MISSING | HIGH | External KMS object constructors could not allocate or withdraw mode identifiers. The managed DRM owner now allocates the lowest free object ID after mode-config initialization, publishes ID/type together, tracks that ID in the same device owner, reuses released IDs, and makes a second unregister a no-op. | `modules::linux_drm::tests::mode_objects_receive_reusable_ids_and_unregister_once`. | B2140-drm-mode-objects |
| FIXED B2142 | MISSING | HIGH | External display drivers had no universal-plane constructor. The managed DRM owner now validates format input, owns a copied format array, allocates the shared KMS object ID, links the plane into the device mode graph, and reverses list/counter/object/allocation state on cleanup or final device release. | `modules::linux_drm::tests::universal_plane_owns_formats_links_the_mode_list_and_cleans_up`. | B2142-drm-universal-plane |
| FIXED B2143 | DEFECT | HIGH | The universal-plane constructor published the CRTC object tag for a plane and leaked its format allocation/object ID if its late publication check failed. The KMS owner now writes the plane tag, rolls both resources back on every failed publication path, and supplies the CRTC constructor/cleanup that links the CRTC into the same graph, assigns its ID/index and legacy primary/cursor plane relation, then reverses that state on teardown. | `modules::linux_drm::tests::{universal_plane_owns_formats_links_the_mode_list_and_cleans_up,crtc_links_legacy_planes_and_reverses_all_owned_state}`. | B2143-drm-crtc-init |
| FIXED B2144 | DEFECT | HIGH | Plane publication had used the encoder-count slot in the mode configuration, so any plane could corrupt encoder numbering. The slots now use their distinct ABI offsets, and the managed KMS owner supplies the encoder constructor/cleanup: typed mode-object ID, owned default name, immutable list/index publication, and full reverse teardown. | `modules::linux_drm::tests::encoder_links_the_mode_graph_with_a_typed_id_and_cleans_up`. | B2144-drm-connector-init |
| FIXED B2145 | MISSING | HIGH | External display drivers had no connector constructor or cleanup path. The managed KMS owner now assigns a typed object ID/index/type, owns the connector name, publishes it in the connector list, and reverses every owned graph edge on teardown. | `modules::linux_drm::tests::connector_links_the_mode_graph_and_cleans_up`. | B2145-drm-kms-split |
| FIXED B2137 | DEFECT | HIGH | PCI BAR reservation and generic managed MMIO reservation kept separate ownership tables, so different driver entry points could claim the same physical range. One resource owner now exports `ioport_resource` and `iomem_resource`, creates busy child claims, rejects overlap beneath the same root, and binds managed claims to device devres release. PCI BAR reservation uses that owner, so a PCI claim and `__devm_request_region` now exclude each other. | `modules::linux_resource::tests::managed_claim_excludes_overlap_and_releases_with_device`; `modules::linux_pci::core::tests::pci_bar_claim_conflicts_with_the_managed_memory_resource_tree`. | B2137-managed-resource-kmi |
| FIXED F991 | DEFECT | HIGH | The Linux PCI facade exported `pci_msix_vec_count` but unconditionally returned `-EINVAL`, preventing any MSI-X-capable external driver from discovering its hardware vector count. It now follows the capability contract: absent capability is `-EINVAL`; otherwise the MSI-X control QSIZE field yields the table count. | `linux_pci::core::tests::msix_vector_count_reads_the_capability_table_size`; modules hosted suite. | F991-pci-msix-vector-count |
| FIXED F994 | DEFECT | HIGH | External `dma_map_page` and page-backed `dma_map_sg` accepted a byte interval beyond the allocation represented by `struct page`, allowing a driver to install an IOMMU mapping outside the page owner’s run. Both now reject overflow and ranges past `PAGE_SIZE << order`; compound-page runs remain valid. | `linux_dma_tests::page_dma_mapping_refuses_a_range_past_the_page_run`; compare `../linux-master/kernel/dma/mapping.c::dma_map_page_attrs`. | F994-iommu-dma-linux-audit |
| FIXED B2086 | DEFECT | HIGH | Virtio-RNG hand-built descriptor zero and every avail/used transition for each entropy read. Its per-device state now owns one `VirtioSplitQueue` from probe through teardown; fills submit the single device-writable bounce segment and consume the shared queue completion length. The driver retains buffer ownership and entropy policy only, while generic transport code owns descriptor allocation, publication, notification, and reclaim. | `drv-virtio-rng` 11/11 including a completion-backed child-specific fill; raw descriptor/avail/used/notify writers are absent from production RNG code. | B2086-virtio-rng-split-queue |
| FIXED B2087 | DEFECT | HIGH | Virtio-sound CONTROLQ hand-built its request/response chain and ring transitions for every control operation. Card installation now creates one persistent shared CONTROLQ owner, and every PCM discovery/configuration/control request submits its two DMA segments and reclaims completion through that owner. EVENTQ and PCM TX/RX queue conversion remain separate active rows because their persistent buffer/repost semantics require distinct owners and completion-token handling. | `drv-virtio-snd` 16/16; production CONTROLQ no longer writes descriptor, avail, used, or notify fields directly. | B2087-virtio-snd-split-queue |
| FIXED B2089 | DEFECT | HIGH | Virtio-sound EVENTQ hand-built its writable descriptors, avail publication, used consumption, buffer repost, and notification. The sound context now owns one persistent `VirtioSplitQueue`; event protocol code consumes completed heads and immediately resubmits the matching event-frame buffer, while the generic owner allocates/retires descriptors and publishes/kicks. | `drv-virtio-snd` event owner tests prepost all slots, emulate completions for two distinct descriptor heads, and verify their shared-owner repost; production EVENTQ code has no raw descriptor/avail/used/notify access. | B2089-virtio-snd-event-owner |
| FIXED B2090 | DEFECT | HIGH | Virtio-sound PCM TXQ and RXQ manually constructed three-descriptor transfer chains and hand-published/consumed their split rings. TX and RX now own persistent `VirtioSplitQueue` instances from install to teardown and submit their header/payload/status segments through the generic owner; synchronous transfer waits consume its completion token and validate the submitted head. | `drv-virtio-snd` 15/15; `virtio` 65/65. Production PCM has no direct descriptor, avail, used, or notify access. Linux `sound/virtio/virtio_pcm_msg.c` uses `virtqueue_add_sgs` and `virtqueue_get_buf` for the same TX/RX contract. | B2090-virtio-snd-pcm-owner |
